<style>
  .hidden {
    display: none;
  }
</style>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
  const openSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.open-search-popup')];
  const closeSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.close-search-popup')];
  const searchPopup = document.querySelector('.search-popup');

  const closePopup = () => {
    searchPopup.style.display = 'none';
    openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.add('hidden'));
  };

  openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(openSearchPopupBtn => {
    openSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', (e) => {
      e.preventDefault();

      searchPopup.style.display = 'block';
      openSearchPopupBtn.classList.add('hidden');
      closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    });
  });

  closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(closeSearchPopupBtn => {
    closeSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', () => {
      closePopup();
    });
  });

  document.addEventListener('click', (event) => {
    if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target) && !openSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target)) && !closeSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target))) {
      setTimeout(() => {
        if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target)) {
          closePopup();
        }
      }, 100);
    }
  });
</script>    </div>

                            <h1 class="header__title title-h2 mb-24 mt-md-48 mt-lg-64 mt-xl-64">FontPack: A dangerous update</h1><p class="header__text mb-xs-48 mb-64 mb-lg-96 mb-xl-96">Attribution secrets: Who is behind stealing credentials and bank card data by asking to install fake Flash Player, browser or font updates?</p><div class="post-info__date mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><div class="body2">June 3, 2021 · <span id="reading-time"></span> min to read · Threat Intelligence</div></div><div class="post-info__image mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><picture><source srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-15.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img data-no-lazy="" fetchpriority="high" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-15.jpg" alt="" class="webpexpress-processed"></picture></div><div class="post-info__tags"><div class="post-info__tag body2">Attribution</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">FontPack</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">Threat Intelligence</div></div>                        </div>
                    </div>
                </div>
                <div class="blog-post-content" id="blog-post-content">
                    <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>Attribution is our main focus here at Group-IB <a href="/products/threat-intelligence/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Threat Intelligence</a>, and it becomes harder every year. The number of unique malicious programs is decreasing while affiliate programs (collaborations between threat actors) are on the rise, with the number and quality of attacks both going up. Today <strong>Nikita Rostovtsev</strong>, an analyst at Group-IB Threat Intelligence, will show you attribution in practice by examining a malicious landing page that Group-IB specialists are tracking as <strong>FontPack</strong>. You will see what this page distributes and how it does so, as well as learn other interesting things that Group-IB has uncovered.</p>
<p>First and foremost we need to find out who is behind the landing page, down to the specific hacking group or particular threat actor. All we know so far is that the page is hosted on compromised websites by injecting JS scripts. The scripts imitate a website crashing and display a message saying that users must update their software, e.g., the browser, Adobe Flash Player, or fonts. The code name used by our team, FontPack, is based on the decoy methods employed in the campaign we will analyze in this report.</p>
<p><strong>Threat actors decide what particular fake to show their victim and how often to do so by changing relevant variables in the script code.</strong> According to our data, multiple unconnected hackers use the tool.</p>
<p>Let us focus on one campaign whose goal was to deliver the RedLine stealer to victim devices. When successful, threat actors were able to collect their victims&#8217; credentials, autocomplete field data, and bank card information. Our analysis revealed that, since November 2020, FontPack has infected at least 20 websites, including six that were involved in one campaign. But first things first.</p>
<h2>What is FontPack?</h2>
<p>By &#8220;landing page&#8221; we mean a target webpage that is shown to users and urges them to download a malicious file that will then be executed. The FontPack landing page has been known to Threat Intelligence researchers since 2018. <a href="https://www.malwarebytes.com/blog/news/2020/02/domen-toolkit-gets-back-to-work-with-new-malvertising-campaign" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">Other specialists</a> may know it as &#8220;Domen toolkit&#8221; because of its variable called &#8220;var domen&#8221;.</p>
<h2>How is FontPack injected into websites?</h2>
<p>Attacks start with injecting FontPack-containing JS scripts into websites created and controlled by the threat actors. Compromised legitimate websites are also used for the same purpose.</p>
<div id="attachment_26016" style="width: 1006px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-16.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26016" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26016 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20996%20976'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="A fake window urging the user to update their browser" width="996" height="976" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-16.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26016" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26016 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-16.png" alt="A fake window urging the user to update their browser" width="996" height="976"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26016" class="wp-caption-text">A fake window urging the user to update their browser, shown on top of a legitimate website</p></div>
<p>Often, victims visit websites that they trust and that they have already been visiting for months, until one day the website asks them to update an outdated plugin. When they do so, malware is downloaded to their computer.<strong> This type of attack is the most effective for threat actors and the most dangerous for regular users because victims do not suspect an infection from a website they trust.</strong> Readers who do not yet understand what such websites can look like might find the gif below helpful:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="video"><a class="video__opener" data-fancybox href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1013BhWPXIg"><div class="button button--md button--filled"><span class="button__icon material-icons"><span class="material-icons">play_arrow</span></span></div></a><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/template.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="" title="template" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/template.jpg"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/template.jpg" alt="" title="template" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>So, what exactly happened there? We see that a user goes to a website that they have visited before. After the victim spends some time there, the website contents starts to visually &#8220;break&#8221; and the browser asks the user to update Flash Player so that everything works properly again. The victim does so.</p>
<h2>Landing page code analysis</h2>
<p>To understand how such attacks work, we looked at the contents of the malicious JS script. By analyzing the website contents, we detected a JS script called &#8220;wp-kernel.js&#8221;.</p>
<div id="attachment_26017" style="width: 1344px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-22.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26017" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26017 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201334%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Example of FontPack landing page code " width="1334" height="563" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-22.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26017" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26017 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-22.png" alt="Example of FontPack landing page code " width="1334" height="563"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26017" class="wp-caption-text">Example of FontPack landing page code showing that a fake website created using it will work both on desktop computers and mobile devices</p></div>
<p>The script contents clearly show that, in addition to personal computers, it can infect mobile devices running Android and Blackberry OS, as well as devices that use the mobile browsers IEMobile and Opera Mini. The script identifies what browser is used for the connection and based on this information provides the victim with a link to the relevant file. The script changes fake windows by changing the value of the variable <em>var banner</em>:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="gallery swiper gallery-swiper-742"><div class="swiper-wrapper"><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-22.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Fake browser update window" title="3-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-22.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-22.png" alt="Fake browser update window" title="3-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text">Fake browser update window</p></div><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-21.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Fake font update window" title="4-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-21.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-21.png" alt="Fake font update window" title="4-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text">Fake font update window</p></div><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-22.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-22.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-22.png" alt="Fake Adobe Flash Player update window" title="5-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text">Fake Adobe Flash Player update window</p></div></div><div class="swiper-pagination"></div><div class="swiper-button-next"></div><div class="swiper-button-prev"></div></div></div>
                 
                  <script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">

                  <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>The variable var startTime sets the time (in milliseconds) after which the user will be shown a fake window.</p>
<p>The variable var linkMobile creates a link to an app for mobile devices (the link is not active in this particular case). As a result, in this particular campaign, we could only identify an infection designed for the Windows operating system.</p>
<p>As mentioned above, when the script is working website contents are visually distorted — this is what the variable var bugs does. For this to happen, the variable must be set to True. When the variable is set to False, there are no changes to the website.</p>
<p>The variable oneTimeShow, sets the frequency with which the script functions. If the variable is set to True, the script will only function once for every user.</p>
<p>In addition, the script contains a set of 27 language systems for which the fake windows will be shown. Spoiler alert: you will not find anything related to the post-Soviet region there.</p>
<p>The aim of the report is not to describe how the script works in detail, however, so let us move on.</p>
<p>The JS script code has the variable var domen with the value browsertelemetry[.]tk. The domain contains an admin panel hosted at https://browsertelemetry[.]tk/admin/login.</p>
<p>The admin panel uses the Cyrillic alphabet on the authentication page:</p>
<div id="attachment_26021" style="width: 717px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-17.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26021" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26021 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20707%20414'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Admin panel used in the FontPack campaign" width="707" height="414" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-17.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26021" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26021 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-17.png" alt="Admin panel used in the FontPack campaign" width="707" height="414"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26021" class="wp-caption-text">Admin panel used in the FontPack campaign</p></div>
<h2>Underground platform profile analysis</h2>
<p>In a report released on February 28, 2020, Malwarebytes researchers showed that the landing page in question was put up for sale on April 10, 2019. In a screenshot provided by the researchers, the thread author is a user with the username xxbtc. The same landing page was distributed by users with the usernames grinGo and holeo. It is noteworthy that the item was put up for sale by three different users on different underground platforms.</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="gallery swiper gallery-swiper-279"><div class="swiper-wrapper"><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-17.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="fontpack landing page in group-ib threat intelligence system" title="7-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-17.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-17.png" alt="fontpack landing page in group-ib threat intelligence system" title="7-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text"></p></div><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-17.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="fontpack landing page in group-ib threat intelligence system" title="8-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-17.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-17.png" alt="fontpack landing page in group-ib threat intelligence system" title="8-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text"></p></div></div><div class="swiper-pagination"></div><div class="swiper-button-next"></div><div class="swiper-button-prev"></div></div></div>

                  <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>Every screenshot above has roughly the same text (with some variation) about selling a Russian-language landing page with the following content:</p>
<p>&#8220;<em>Script for achieving EXE/APK installations!</em><br />
<em>The script is designed for achieving installations from websites/shells/fakes</em><br />
<em>It is adapted to both computers and mobile devices</em><br />
<em>The JS script connects to any website through &lt;script&gt;</em>&#8221;</p>
<p>Interestingly, six days later xxbtc invited forum users to test MagBo, an exchange for trading shells. Let us note this fact and return to it later.</p>
<div id="attachment_26024" style="width: 1573px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-12.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26024" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26024 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201563%20674'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="MagBo scam message in group-ib threat intelligence system" width="1563" height="674" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-12.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26024" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26024 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-12.png" alt="MagBo scam message in group-ib threat intelligence system" width="1563" height="674"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26024" class="wp-caption-text">MagBo is slowly entering our story…</p></div>
<p>The user shared an invite code: CTXDDYMGFJ. According to our data, six users on various platforms posted similar messages with the same invite code.</p>
<p><em>Xxtbc, xenys, gonleen, amlogic, grinGo, and pacificcc.</em></p>
<p>In addition, a uniting factor for some of these usernames are Jabber and Telegram mentioned for communication purposes.</p>
<p><a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection" class="__cf_email__" data-cfemail="8af2f2f2e8fee9caeff2fae6e5e3fea4e3e7">[email&#160;protected]</a> and @xengf</p>
<p><em>Xxbtc, kista, holeo, xenys, amlogic, exynos, gringo, and pacificcc.</em></p>
<div id="attachment_26025" style="width: 1605px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-14.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26025" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26025 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201595%20387'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Telegram account associated with fontpack scam" width="1595" height="387" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-14.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26025" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26025 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-14.png" alt="Telegram account associated with fontpack scam" width="1595" height="387"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26025" class="wp-caption-text">The same Telegram account associated with different usernames. &#8220;For the last two days of the offer, the price is $50. Telegram @xengf. A big set for a very low price!&#8221;</p></div>
<p>It is highly likely that the above means that these accounts belong to one person. Below is a mind map we created that makes our conclusion clearer.</p>
<div id="attachment_26026" style="width: 1510px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-min-11.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26026" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26026 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201500%20807'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Interrelations between different fontpack profiles" width="1500" height="807" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-min-11.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26026" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26026 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-min-11.png" alt="Interrelations between different fontpack profiles" width="1500" height="807"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26026" class="wp-caption-text">Interrelations between different profiles that highly likely belong to one person</p></div>
<h2>How does MagBo factor in?</h2>
<p>According to our data, since November 2020 the landing page in question has infected at least 20 websites, including six involved in one campaign, i.e. linked to the domain browsertelemetry[.]tk. It is worth noting that access to some of these websites was sold on MagBo. In addition, posts made by xxxbtc on MagBo included messages about selling logs for November–December 2020.</p>
<div id="attachment_26027" style="width: 674px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/12-min-10.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26027" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26027 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20664%20503'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Logs sold on MagBo" width="664" height="503" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/12-min-10.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26027" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26027 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/12-min-10.png" alt="Logs sold on MagBo" width="664" height="503"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26027" class="wp-caption-text">Logs sold on MagBo by a user we are already familiar with: хххbtc. &#8220;Over 6,000 logs for November December. 800-1,200 logs had cryptos extracted – the rest hasn&#8217;t been touched and hasn&#8217;t even been checked&#8221;</p></div>
<h2>So who is the author of the landing page?</h2>
<p>A more in-depth analysis revealed that the landing page we call FontPack was put up for sale by a seller with the username DR.PREDATOR in January 2018.</p>
<div id="attachment_26028" style="width: 1562px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/13-min-8.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26028" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26028 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201552%20544'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Historical data of fontpack fake landing page" width="1552" height="544" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/13-min-8.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26028" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26028 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/13-min-8.png" alt="Historical data of fontpack fake landing page" width="1552" height="544"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26028" class="wp-caption-text">Historical data from our TI system: the landing page in question was first put up for sale as early as January 2018.</p></div>
<p>In late 2019, the project was shut down and made publicly available, which meant that anyone could download it for free.</p>
<div id="attachment_26008" style="width: 1536px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/14-min-11.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26008" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26008 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201526%20887'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Historical data of fontpack fake landing page" width="1526" height="887" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/14-min-11.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26008" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26008 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/14-min-11.png" alt="Historical data of fontpack fake landing page" width="1526" height="887"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26008" class="wp-caption-text">In late 2019, the user DR.PREDATOR made the landing page publicly available. &#8220;Because the service has closed (…), I would like to publish Install Pack (with no obfuscation and binding) + Instructions in an archive (…) Enjoy, because some have already started reselling it&#8221;</p></div>
<p>The structure of the published project is extremely similar to what xxbtc offers. Since October 10, 2019, however, xxbtc has been offering a set that includes the script from DR.PREDATOR.</p>
<div id="attachment_26009" style="width: 1555px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min-10.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26009" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26009 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201545%20615'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Historical data of fontpack fake landing page" width="1545" height="615" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min-10.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26009" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26009 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min-10.png" alt="Historical data of fontpack fake landing page" width="1545" height="615"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26009" class="wp-caption-text">An amazing coincidence: xxbtc also published a pack that included DR.PREDATOR&#8217;s script. &#8220;Scam websites for delivering your exe! LAST DAYS OF THE OFFER. I AM SELLING A SET OF 15 websites for delivering exe with different topics + a script for exe/apk from dr.predator (&#8230;) Telegram @xengf</p></div>

<p>The campaign involves the following domains:</p>

                  <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>The download links led to a file and a malware-containing archive.</p>
<ul>
<li>FlashPlayer.exe (from the zip file) – SHA1 1ea09cd229b34951007f81c8e5acd323386e4fb6</li>
<li>FlashPlayer.exe &#8211; SHA1 36d08c8ab8e161923403cd89bdf3600fccd6629a</li>
</ul>
<p>Detonation in Group-IB Managed XDR Polygon, our system for launching malware in an isolated environment, revealed that these files are samples of RedLine Stealer.</p>
<div id="attachment_26012" style="width: 1558px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min-1.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26012" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26012 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201548%20922'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="screenshot with the result of detonating the redline stealer files" width="1548" height="922" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min-1.jpg"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26012" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26012 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min-1.jpg" alt="screenshot with the result of detonating the redline stealer files" width="1548" height="922"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26012" class="wp-caption-text">Identifying the type of malware: The screenshot shows the result of detonating the files in Group-IB Managed XDR Polygon, our system for launching malware in an isolated environment. The files are RedLine Stealer samples.</p></div>
<p>When executed, the files send the following type of HTTP requests to their command-and-control (C&amp;C) server:</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/19-min-7.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone wp-image-26013 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20803%20777'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="redline stealer HTTP requests" width="803" height="777" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/19-min-7.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone wp-image-26013 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/19-min-7.png" alt="redline stealer HTTP requests" width="803" height="777"></noscript></picture></p>
<p><strong>In summary, so far our analysis revealed that:</strong></p>
<ul>
<li>The FontPack landing page is a set of fake webpages designed for tricking users into downloading a malicious file.</li>
<li>FontPack is distributed as a JS script.</li>
<li>The JS script can be installed on a compromised or threat actor-controlled server.</li>
<li>FontPack contains fakes mimicking a browser, font or Adobe Flash Player update.</li>
<li>FontPack works on both desktops and mobile browsers.</li>
<li>The aim of the landing page is to determine what browser version is used and, based on that information, to provide a link for downloading a particular file.</li>
<li>One of the analyzed campaigns involved delivering RedLine Stealer.</li>
</ul>
<h2>What is RedLine Stealer capable of?</h2>
<p>The hacker community learned about <strong>RedLine Stealer</strong> in early 2020, when it was put up for sale for the first time on multiple underground forums.</p>
<p>RedLine quickly became popular and has involved over 230 C&amp;C servers since October 2020 according to our data. The stealer is written in C# and its functionality is typical for this type of malware:</p>
<ol>
<li>It collects credentials, cookies, autocomplete field data, and credit card information from all Chromium/Gecko-based browsers</li>
<li>It collects data from FTP and IM clients</li>
<li>It identifies countries where the stealer will not function</li>
<li>It collects information about the victim&#8217;s PC</li>
<li>It manages anti-duplicate logs settings in the admin panel</li>
<li>It deletes itself</li>
<li>It performs tasks in four different ways.</li>
</ol>
<p>These ways are:</p>
<ol>
<li>It downloads a file to a specified path through a direct link</li>
<li>It injects a 32-bit file downloaded through a direct link into another file, which must be specified</li>
<li>It downloads a file to a specified path through a direct link and later launches it</li>
<li>It opens a link in the default browser</li>
</ol>
<p>Interestingly, websites compromised as early as February 2021 still deliver payloads despite the stealer&#8217;s C&amp;C server no longer being available.</p>
<p>As a closing remark, the campaign is only one of many that involve this landing page. The fake updates are especially interesting considering that Flash Player support was discontinued in early 2021.</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-1.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-26015 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201500%201675'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="fontpack mitre att&ck and ttps" width="1500" height="1675" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-1.jpg"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-26015 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-1.jpg" alt="fontpack mitre att&ck and ttps" width="1500" height="1675"></noscript></picture></p>

    const postcontent = document.getElementById('blog-post-content');
    const h2counter = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2');

    for (i = 0; i < h2counter.length; i++) {
        const h2elementtag = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i];
        const h2element = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i].innerHTML;
        let postnavitem = document.createElement('a');
        postnavitem.classList.add('post-navigation__item', 'subtitle2', 'scrollto');
        postnavitem.innerHTML = h2element;
        const postnavlink = postnavitem.innerHTML;
        let str = postnavlink;
        str = str.replace(/<[^>]*>/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9\s\u0400-\u04FF]/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/\s+/g, '-').toLowerCase();
        str = str.replace(/^-+|-+$/g, '');

        postnavitem.href = '#' + str;
        h2elementtag.id = str;
        document.getElementById('post-navigation__wrapper').appendChild(postnavitem);
    }

    const sections = document.querySelectorAll('h2');
    const navItems = document.querySelectorAll('.post-navigation__item');

    window.addEventListener('scroll', () => {
        let current = '';
        sections.forEach((section) => {
            const sectionTop = section.offsetTop;
            const sectionHeight = section.clientHeight;
            if (pageYOffset >= sectionTop - sectionHeight / 10) {
                current = section.getAttribute('id');
            }
        });

        navItems.forEach((a) => {
            a.classList.remove('active');
            if (a.classList.contains(current)) {
                a.classList.add('active');
            }
        });
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrollto').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300,
        );

        return false;
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL WITH NO HASH
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrolltonohash').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300
        );

        return false;
    });
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    function readingTime() {
        const text = document.getElementById("blog-post-content").innerText;
        const wpm = 225;
        const words = text.trim().split(/\s+/).length;
        const time = Math.ceil(words / wpm);
        document.getElementById("reading-time").innerText = time;
    }
    readingTime();
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    document.getElementById("share-url").value = window.location.href;
    document.getElementById("share-url-mobile").value = window.location.href;
    jQuery('.share-url__copy').click(function() {
        jQuery('.share-url__copy').addClass('success');
        navigator.clipboard.writeText(window.location.href);
        setTimeout(() => {
            jQuery('.share-url__copy').removeClass('success');
        }, 1500);
    });
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    var carousel123 = new Swiper('.carousel123', {
        grabCursor: true,
        pagination: {
            el: '.swiper-pagination',
            clickable: true,
        },
    });

    var authorsMoreThan2Swiper = new Swiper('.authors-more-than-2-swiper', {
        grabCursor: true,
        pagination: {
            el: '.swiper-pagination',
            clickable: true,
        },
        breakpoints: {
            0: {
                slidesPerView: 'auto',
                spaceBetween: 16,
                slidesPerView: 1,
                slidesPerGroup: 1,
            },
            620: {
                spaceBetween: 24,
                slidesPerView: 2,
                slidesPerGroup: 2,
            },
        },
    });

    function positioningSidebar() {
        let sidebar = document.getElementById('sidebar');
        let sidebarHeight = sidebar.offsetHeight;
        let windowHeight = document.documentElement.clientHeight;
        if (windowHeight < (sidebarHeight + 48)) {
            let sidebarAuthors = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__authors');
            let sidebarMorePosts = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__more-posts');
            let authorsHeight = sidebarAuthors ? sidebarAuthors.offsetHeight : 0;
            let morePostsHeight = sidebarMorePosts ? sidebarMorePosts.offsetHeight : 0;
            let sidebarTop = authorsHeight + morePostsHeight + 140;
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', -sidebarTop + 'px');
        } else {
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', '72px');
        }
    }
    window.addEventListener('resize', positioningSidebar);
    positioningSidebar();

    if (localStorage.getItem('theme')) {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
        } else if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'light') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--white');
        }
    } else {
        localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
    }

    jQuery('.theme-toggle').click(function() {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'light');
        } else {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        }
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--dark');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--white');
        setTimeout(() => {
            jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        }, 250);
    });
    jQuery('.share-toggle').click(function() {
        jQuery('.share-toggle').toggleClass('active');
        jQuery('.share-widget').toggleClass('show');
    });
</script>
